   952	/// An authored equality may already be the right authorization scope while a
   953	/// named order index leaves it as a residual filter. Print the two exact edits
   954	/// together: the compatible declaration and the same query selecting it.
   955	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — equality-filter advice is mechanically coherent even when it remains advisory.
   956	fn equality_filter_index_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table,
   957	    scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan, unpinned: &[String], leading: &[String],
   958	    declaration_site: &crate::Site, read_site: Option<&crate::Site>) -> Option<String> {
   959	    use snapback4_core::ir::{BinaryOp, Expr};
   960	    fn pins(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
   961	        match expr {
   962	            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left, right } => pins(left, column) || pins(right, column),
   963	            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left, right } => field(left, column) && independent(right)
   964	                || field(right, column) && independent(left),
   965	            _ => false,
   966	        }
   967	    }
   968	    fn field(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
   969	        matches!(expr, Expr::Field { base, name } if name == column
   970	            && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter"))
   971	    }
   972	    fn independent(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
   973	        match expr {
   974	            Expr::Field { base, .. } => !matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter") && independent(base),
   975	            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => independent(left) && independent(right),
   976	            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => independent(value),
   977	            Expr::Array(values) => values.iter().all(independent),
   978	            Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().all(independent),
   979	            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().all(independent),
   980	            Expr::Get(_) | Expr::Exists(_) | Expr::Scan(_) | Expr::Merge(_) | Expr::Block { .. } => false,
   981	            _ => true,
   982	        }
   983	    }
   984	    let filter = scan.filter.as_deref()?;
   985	    if unpinned.is_empty() || leading.len() > 8 || !unpinned.iter().all(|column| pins(filter, column)) { return None; }
   986	    let title = |value: &str| { let mut chars = value.chars(); chars.next()
   987	        .map(|first| first.to_uppercase().collect::<String>() + chars.as_str()).unwrap_or_default() };
   988	    let stem = leading.iter().filter(|column| column.as_str() != "id").map(|column| title(column)).collect::<String>();
   989	    if stem.is_empty() { return None; }
   990	    let mut name = format!("by{stem}");
   991	    let mut suffix = 2;
   992	    while table.indexes.contains_key(&name) {
   993	        name = format!("by{stem}{suffix}"); suffix += 1;
   994	    }
   995	    let authored = catalog.tables.get(&scan.table)?;
   996	    let candidate = crate::ast::IndexDecl { name: name.clone(), columns: leading.to_vec(), unique: false,
   997	        refusal: None, words: None, computed: Default::default(), site: declaration_site.clone() };
   998	    if !key_bound::plain_index_fits(authored, &candidate, &name) { return None; }
   999	    // Locate the authored read by its source-map span. A declaration can use
  1000	    // the same old index more than once; replacing a text occurrence can edit
  1001	    // an earlier, unrelated read and print a query that does not compile.
  1002	    let read_site = read_site?;
  1003	    let (mut read, text, grouped, start) = catalog.source.read(read_site)?;
  1004	    if read.index.as_deref() != Some(scan.index.as_str()) { return None; }
  1005	    read.index = Some(name.clone());
  1006	    let fixed = crate::ast::Expr::Read(read);
  1007	    let replacement = crate::print::with_source_at(&fixed, if grouped { 8 } else { 0 }, &|expr| catalog.source.get(expr));
  1008	    let declaration = catalog.source.declarations.get(&declaration_site.to_string())?;
  1009	    let line = start.line.checked_sub(declaration_site.line)? as usize;
  1010	    let lines = declaration.split('\n').collect::<Vec<_>>();
  1011	    let column = start.col.checked_sub(1)? as usize;
  1012	    let byte = lines.get(line)?.char_indices().map(|(i, _)| i).chain([lines[line].len()]).nth(column)?;
  1013	    let offset = lines[..line].iter().map(|line| line.len() + 1).sum::<usize>() + byte;
  1014	    let span = offset..offset.checked_add(text.len())?;
  1015	    if declaration.get(span.clone())? != text { return None; }
  1016	    let rewritten = crate::parser::repairs::splice(declaration, vec![(span, replacement)])?;
  1017	    Some(format!("on table {} add `by {name}: {}`; use the same index in the rewritten query:\n```quarry\n{rewritten}\n```", scan.table, leading.join(", ")))
  1018	}
  1019	
  1020	fn matching_merge_rewrite(schema: &Schema, scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan, table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table) -> Option<String> {
  1021	    use snapback4_core::{ir::IndexKeyExpr, schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm}};
  1022	    if !scan.prefix.iter().any(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_))) { return None; }
  1023	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — a membership conjunct supplies lanes when their scope decides the whole rule.
  1024	    fn conjuncts(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
  1025	        match rule { RuleExpr::And(terms) => terms.iter().flat_map(conjuncts).collect(), other => vec![other] }
  1026	    }
  1027	    conjuncts(&table.rules.read).into_iter().find_map(|rule| {
  1028	        let RuleExpr::Exists { table: members, index: membership_index, key, .. } = rule else { return None; };
  1029	        let membership = schema.table(members)?;
  1030	        let columns = membership.index(membership_index)?.columns();
  1031	        let principal = key.iter().position(|term| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer))?;
  1032	        let scope: Vec<_> = key.iter().filter_map(|term| if let RuleTerm::Field(field) = term { Some(field.clone()) } else { None }).collect();
  1033	        let deciding = snapback4_core::engine::rules::deciding_fields(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read);
  1034	        if !deciding.iter().all(|field| scope.contains(field)) { return None; }
  1035	        let fields = table.index(&scan.index)?.word_component()?.1;
  1036	        let (_, words_index) = table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| index.word_component().is_some_and(|(position, text)| index.columns()[..position] == scope && text == fields))?;
  1037	        let _ = membership.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| index.is_plain() && index.columns().first() == columns.get(principal) && index.columns().last().is_some_and(|column| column == "id"))?;
  1038	        let order = words_index.columns().into_iter().skip(scope.len()).map(|column| format!(".{column}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
     1	//! N_NEVER_COMPLETE names the missing pin as code against the author's own
     2	//! columns: the equality to add, the parent read that supplies its value, the
     3	//! two-query split for a two-party rule, and whether a role grant is used.
     4	// @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — app-farm patterns 6 and 28.
     5	
     6	use snapback4_core::ir::{ArgType, Expr, Program, Scan};
     7	use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema, Table};
     8	
     9	/// The concrete repair for a page whose read rule reads `unpinned`, or None
    10	/// when no rendering beats the generic advice: the code to paste first (the
    11	/// author's query split by role or by party, when it can be rendered from the
    12	/// declaration), then the rest of the advice.
    13	// @ref LLP 3000.036#14.2 — the role branch or per-party split leads the note, as code that compiles.
    14	pub(super) fn pin_rewrite(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, scan: &Scan, table: &Table, unpinned: &[String], declaration: Option<&str>) -> Option<(Option<String>, String)> {
    15	    let rule = &table.rules.read;
    16	    let disjuncts = rule.disjuncts();
    17	    let viewer_column = |rule: &RuleExpr| match rule {
    18	        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) => Some(column.clone()),
    19	        _ => None,
    20	    };
    21	    let parties: Vec<String> = disjuncts.iter().filter_map(|d| viewer_column(d)).collect();
    22	    let name = &scan.table;
    23	    if let Some(repair) = hidden_parent_repair(schema, &scan.table, table) {
    24	        return Some((Some(repair.clone()), format!("the parent-owner witness is not readable to every owner, so discovery cannot certify absence; {repair}")));
    25	    }
    26	    let grant = snapback4_core::engine::rules::grant_decision(schema, scan);
    27	    let mut out = Vec::new();
    28	    // @ref LLP 3000.036#16.1 — the form is built from every disjunct and printed only when it admits exactly the rule's readers.
    29	    let form = super::complete_form::complete_form(schema, program, scan, table, declaration);
    30	    let code = match &form { super::complete_form::Outcome::Form(form) => Some(format!("{}\n{}", form.lead, form.code)), _ => None };
    31	    let only_parties = parties.len() >= 2 && unpinned.iter().all(|column| parties.contains(column)) && disjuncts.len() == parties.len();
    32	    if only_parties {
    33	        let queries = parties.iter().map(|column| format!("`{name} where .{column} = viewer …`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
    34	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — wave 2 #11: print the author's own query once per party, ready to paste.
    35	        if code.is_some() {
    36	            out.push(format!("`{}` admits either party, so one page cannot certify both (an `or` of the two pins in `where` is still one scan)", rule.quarry()));
    37	        } else {
    38	            out.push(format!("`{}` admits either party, so one page cannot certify both. Declare one query per party, each pinned and complete: {queries} (same order, page and cursor), and show or merge them in the client", rule.quarry()));
    39	        }
    40	    } else {
    41	        let mut setup = Vec::new();
    42	        let mut pins = Vec::new();
    43	        for column in unpinned {
    44	            let (pin, line) = pin_for(schema, program, scan, table, column);
    45	            pins.push(pin);
    46	            if let Some(line) = line { if !setup.contains(&line) { setup.push(line); } }
    47	        }
    48	        let first = if setup.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("first {}, then ", setup.join("; ")) };
    49	        let others = if disjuncts.len() > 1 { " — only for a screen meant for that scope alone: every other reader the rule admits loses their rows" } else { "" };
    50	        out.push(format!("{first}add `and {}` to the page's where (keeping its current pins); this narrows the answer to that scope{others}", pins.join(" and ")));
    51	    }
    52	    if let super::complete_form::Outcome::None(why) = &form {
    53	        out.push(format!("analyzer limitation: no verified complete form for this read: {why}; keep the query as written and expect `complete: false` (`capped`; test.cases.jsonl's default passes it). This does not rule out an author-written branched query"));
    54	    }
    55	    // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — the engine decides a page once for a caller whose readable grant holds.
    56	    if let Some(used) = &grant {
    57	        out.push(format!("for callers `{}` admits (staff), this page is decided once and answers `complete: true`; everyone else pages their own rows with complete: false — in test.cases.jsonl run it as staff to expect complete, and expect `capped` for the others", used.quarry()));
    58	    } else if let Some(grant) = disjuncts.iter().find(|d| row_independent(d)) {
    59	        out.push(format!("the grant `{}` reads no row field, but its holders cannot read the row that grants it, so it does not decide the page (that would disclose the hidden row): they receive every row with complete: false. Let each holder read their own grant row (`read <- .principal = viewer` on the probed table) to make their pages complete", grant.quarry()));
    60	    }
    61	    // @ref LLP 3000.036#16.3 — a disjunct the pins decide is a page guard for its holders, as a grant is.
    62	    let pinned: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = table.index(&scan.index).map(|index| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(component, key)| key.scalar().and(component.column()).cloned()).collect()).unwrap_or_default();
    63	    for decided in prefix_decided(table, &pinned) {
    64	        if snapback4_core::engine::rules::pinned_probe_readable(schema, &decided) {
    65	            out.push(format!("for callers `{}` admits with the pinned values, this page is decided once and answers `complete: true` (an `if` or `require` on that probe is followed)", decided.quarry()));
    66	        } else {
    67	            out.push(format!("`{}` is decided by the scan's pins, but its holders cannot read the row it probes, so it does not decide the page (that would disclose the hidden row): they receive every row with complete: false", decided.quarry()));
    68	        }
    69	    }
    70	    Some((code, out.join("; ")))
    71	}
    72	
    73	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#16 — an unreadable immutable parent owner needs an exact checked column copy, never a public parent.
    74	pub(super) fn hidden_parent_repair(schema: &Schema, name: &str, table: &Table) -> Option<String> {
    75	    let probe = table.rules.read.disjuncts().into_iter().find_map(|rule| {
    76	        let RuleExpr::Exists { table: parent, index, key, equalities, .. } = rule else { return None };
    77	        if snapback4_core::engine::rules::pinned_probe_readable(schema, rule) { return None; }
    78	        let [RuleTerm::Field(reference)] = key.as_slice() else { return None };
    79	        let [(owner, RuleTerm::Viewer)] = equalities.as_slice() else { return None };
    80	        let source = schema.table(parent)?;
    81	        if source.index(index)?.columns() != ["id"] || !source.immutable_fields().any(|field| field == owner)
    82	            || source.read_lifetime_sensitive() || source.through.is_some()
    83	            || table.on_delete.get(reference).is_some_and(|action| !matches!(action, snapback4_core::schema::references::OnDelete::Restrict | snapback4_core::schema::references::OnDelete::Cascade))
    84	            || source.columns.get(owner) != Some(&ColumnType::Principal)
    85	            || !matches!(table.columns.get(reference), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table }) if table == parent) { return None; }
    86	        Some((rule, parent, reference, owner))
    87	    })?;
    88	    let (rule, parent, reference, owner) = probe;
    89	    let mut chars = owner.chars();
    90	    let copy = format!("{reference}{}{}", chars.next()?.to_uppercase(), chars.as_str());
    91	    if table.columns.contains_key(&copy) { return None; }
    92	    let read = table.rules.read.quarry().replace(&rule.quarry(), &format!(".{copy} = viewer"));
    93	    Some(format!("on table {} declare `{reference}: {parent} on delete restrict`, add `{copy}: principal` and `immutable {reference}, {copy}`; replace its read declaration with `read <- {read}`; replace its insert declaration with `insert <- ({}) and exists {parent}[.{reference}, {owner} = .{copy}]`; in every `insert {} {{ … }}` payload add `{copy}: {copy}Source.{owner}` after inserting `{copy}Source = {parent}[{reference}] ! else NOT_FOUND` immediately before that insert (using the same reference value supplied in its payload), and backfill that field from {parent}.{owner} in existing rows and trusted seeds before adoption. The parent declares `immutable {owner}`, so this preserves the original readers; a mutable parent owner instead requires transactional copy propagation", name, table.rules.insert.quarry(), name))
    94	}
    95	
    96	/// The page statement of a one-statement query declaration: its `return`'s
    97	/// read over `table`, with the rest of the declaration's header.
    98	pub(super) fn single_page(text: &str, table: &str) -> Option<(String, crate::ast::ProgramDecl, crate::ast::Expr)> {
    99	    use crate::ast::{Expr as A, Stmt as S};
   100	    let (document, errors) = crate::parser::parse_document("query.q", text);
   101	    if !errors.is_empty() { return None; }
   102	    let [decl] = document.programs.as_slice() else { return None };
   103	    let [S::Return { value, .. }] = decl.body.as_slice() else { return None };
   104	    if !decl.prelude.is_empty() { return None; }
   105	    let read = match value {
   106	        A::Read(read) => read,
   107	        A::Shape { base, .. } | A::ShapeUse { base, .. } => match base.as_ref() { A::Read(read) => read, _ => return None },
   108	        _ => return None,
   109	    };
   110	    if read.table != table || !read.keys.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some() || read.index.is_some() { return None; }
   111	    let (header, _) = text.split_once('\n')?;
   112	    Some((header.to_owned(), decl.clone(), value.clone()))
   113	}
   114	
   115	/// `value` (a page over `table`, maybe shaped) with its `where` keeping every
   116	/// conjunct but the parties' pins and their disjunction, and `.party = viewer` first.
   117	pub(super) fn pinned_to(value: &crate::ast::Expr, parties: &[String], party: &str) -> Option<crate::ast::Expr> {
   118	    use crate::ast::Expr as A;
   119	    let mut value = value.clone();
   120	    let read = match &mut value {
   121	        A::Read(read) => read,
   122	        A::Shape { base, .. } | A::ShapeUse { base, .. } => match base.as_mut() { A::Read(read) => read, _ => return None },
   123	        _ => return None,
   124	    };
   125	    let site = read.site.clone();
   126	    let pin = |column: &str, site: &crate::Site| A::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(A::Member { base: Box::new(A::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: column.into(), site: site.clone() }), right: Box::new(A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: site.clone() }), site: site.clone() };
   127	    let party_only = |expr: &A| {
   128	        fn disjuncts<'a>(e: &'a A, out: &mut Vec<&'a A>) { match e { A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => { disjuncts(left, out); disjuncts(right, out); }, other => out.push(other) } }
   129	        let mut parts = Vec::new();
   130	        disjuncts(expr, &mut parts);
   131	        parts.len() > 1 && parts.iter().all(|part| matches!(part, A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" && matches!(right.as_ref(), A::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer") && matches!(left.as_ref(), A::Member { base, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Focal { .. }))))
   132	    };
   133	    let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
   134	    fn split(e: A, out: &mut Vec<A>) { match e { A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => { split(*left, out); split(*right, out); }, other => out.push(other) } }
   135	    if let Some(filter) = read.filter.take() { split(*filter, &mut conjuncts); }
   136	    conjuncts.retain(|part| !party_only(part) && !matches!(part, A::Binary { op, left, .. } if op == "=" && matches!(left.as_ref(), A::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Focal { .. }) && parties.iter().any(|p| p == name))));
   137	    let filter = conjuncts.into_iter().fold(pin(party, &site), |acc, part| A::Binary { op: "and".into(), left: Box::new(acc), right: Box::new(part), site: site.clone() });
   138	    read.filter = Some(Box::new(filter));
   139	    Some(value)
   140	}
   141	
   142	/// `exists staff[viewer]` or `viewer = 'service:x'`: the same for every row.
   143	fn row_independent(rule: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   144	    let mut fields = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
   145	    rule.fields(&mut fields);
   146	    fields.is_empty() && !matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny | RuleExpr::Public { .. })
   147	}
   148	
   149	fn pin_for(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, scan: &Scan, table: &Table, column: &str) -> (String, Option<String>) {
   150	    if let RuleExpr::Not(inner) = &table.rules.read
   151	        && matches!(table.columns.get(column), Some(ColumnType::Bool))
   152	        && let RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(field), RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Bool(expected))) = inner.as_ref()
   153	        && field == column { return (format!(".{column} = {}", !expected), None); }
   154	    let viewer = matches!(&table.rules.read, rule if contains_viewer_eq(rule, column));
   155	    if viewer { return (format!(".{column} = viewer"), None); }
   156	    let kind = table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required);
   157	    // A reference argument's readable row may supply the exact extra pin.
   158	    // This is source advice, not a proof that copied child fields equal it.
   159	    for (argument, ty) in &program.args {
   160	        let ArgType::Ref { table: parent } = ty else { continue };
   161	        let Some(parent_table) = schema.table(parent) else { continue };
   162	        if column != "id" && parent_table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required) == kind && kind.is_some()
   163	            && table.columns.values().any(|ty| matches!(ty.required(), ColumnType::Ref { table } if table == parent)) {
   164	            let local = format!("{argument}Scope");
   165	            return (format!(".{column} = {local}.{column}"), Some(format!("`{local} = {parent}[{argument}] ! else NOT_FOUND`")));
   166	        }
   167	    }
   168	    // A pinned reference whose row carries the same parent: `.board = c.board`.
   169	    if let Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: parent }) = kind
   170	        && let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) {
   171	        for (part, key) in index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix) {
   172	            let (Some(pinned), Some(Expr::Variable(value))) = (part.column(), key.scalar()) else { continue };
   173	            if value.starts_with('$') { continue; }
   174	            let Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: via }) = table.columns.get(pinned).map(ColumnType::required) else { continue };
   175	            let Some(via_table) = schema.table(via) else { continue };
   176	            if let Some((field, _)) = via_table.columns.iter().find(|(_, ty)| matches!(ty, ColumnType::Ref { table } if table == parent)) {
   177	                let local = format!("{}Row", value);
   178	                return (format!(".{column} = {local}.{field}"), Some(format!("read its parent: `{local} = {via}[{value}] ! else NOT_FOUND`")));
   179	            }
   180	        }
   181	    }
   182	    // An argument of the column's type.
   183	    let wanted = match kind {
   184	        Some(ColumnType::Ref { table }) => Some(ArgType::Ref { table: table.clone() }),
   185	        Some(ColumnType::Id) => Some(ArgType::Id),
   186	        Some(ColumnType::Enum { variants }) => Some(ArgType::Enum { variants: variants.clone() }),
   187	        Some(ColumnType::Principal) => Some(ArgType::Principal),
   188	        Some(ColumnType::Int) => Some(ArgType::Int),
   189	        Some(ColumnType::IntRange { min, max }) => Some(ArgType::IntRange { min: *min, max: *max }),
   190	        Some(ColumnType::Time) => Some(ArgType::Time),
   191	        _ => None,
   192	    };
   193	    if let Some(wanted) = wanted && let Some((arg, _)) = program.args.iter().find(|(_, ty)| **ty == wanted) {
   194	        return (format!(".{column} = {arg}"), None);
   195	    }
   196	    let declared = match kind {
   197	        Some(ColumnType::Ref { table }) => table.clone(),
   198	        Some(ColumnType::Principal) => "principal".into(),
   199	        Some(ColumnType::Int) => "int".into(),
   200	        Some(kind @ ColumnType::IntRange { .. }) => kind.to_string(),
   201	        Some(ColumnType::Time) => "time".into(),
   202	        Some(kind) => kind.to_string(),
   203	        _ => "…".into(),
   204	    };
   205	    (format!(".{column} = {column}"), Some(format!("declare `{column}: {declared}` among {}'s arguments", program.name)))
   206	}
   207	
   208	fn contains_viewer_eq(rule: &RuleExpr, column: &str) -> bool {
   209	    match rule {
   210	        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(c), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(c)) => c == column,
   211	        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|item| contains_viewer_eq(item, column)),
   212	        _ => false,
   213	    }
   214	}

    89	    fn index(&mut self, table: &TableInfo, columns: Vec<String>, words: Option<(usize, Vec<String>)>, site: &Site, exact: bool) -> String {
    90	        self.index_computed(table, columns, words, BTreeMap::new(), site, exact)

    93	    fn index_computed(&mut self, table: &TableInfo, columns: Vec<String>, words: Option<(usize, Vec<String>)>, computed: BTreeMap<String, Expr>, site: &Site, exact: bool) -> String {
    94	        let computed_key = computed_descriptor(table, &computed);
    95	        let mut matching = table.indexes.iter().filter(|index| computed_descriptor(table, &index.computed) == computed_key && index.words == words
    96	            && if exact { index.columns == columns } else { index.columns.starts_with(&columns) }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    97	        matching.sort_by_key(|index| &index.name);
    98	        if exact && matching.len() > 1 {
    99	            self.errors.push(crate::diag::ambiguous(site, format!("{} has several declared indexes for the same planned read: {}", table.decl.name, matching.iter().map(|index| index.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
   100	                format!("choose `by {}` explicitly or keep one definition", matching[0].name)));
   101	        }
   102	        if let Some(index) = matching.first() { return index.name.clone(); }
   103	        // A structural request token is replaced only after all requests have
   104	        // been sorted. Collisions therefore cannot depend on program order.
   105	        let token = format!("$intent:{}", snapback4_core::value::digest(&(&columns, &words, &computed_key, false)));
   106	        self.indexes.entry((table.decl.name.clone(), token.clone())).or_insert_with(|| IndexDecl {
   107	            name: token.clone(), columns, words, computed, unique: false, refusal: None, site: site.clone(),
   108	        });
   109	        token
   110	    }

   273	    fn selected_prefix(&self, table: &TableInfo, read: &mut ReadExpr) {
   274	        let Some(index) = table.indexes.iter().find(|index| Some(&index.name) == read.index.as_ref()) else { return; };

   285	            // A range needs an unpinned component. Existing seeks take the
   286	            // unchanged binder path above, including originally invalid seeks.
   287	            if read.keys.len() + 1 == index.columns.len() { break; }
   288	            if !table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.optional) { break; }

   290	                if !matches!(predicate, Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "=") { return None; }

   246	    fn open_branch_lane<'a>(&mut self, lane: &'a MergeLane, prefix: Vec<Value>, after: Option<&(Vec<Value>, bool)>, grant: Option<super::super::expiry::Pending>, metered: bool) -> Result<Lane<'a>, RuntimeError> {

   250	        // Scan merely to clear it; evaluate only the remaining bounds here.

   252	        if metered && let Some((values, _)) = after { self.meter.heap(DisclosedKeys(values).heap())?; }

   254	        let mut done = false;
   255	        if point_id {
   256	            // The point's pinned id is also its logical page position. Decide
   257	            // the shared cursor here instead of applying it after the fully
   258	            // consumed physical prefix.

   273	    /// Read a lane's next batch strictly after its position, recording how

  1082	            words: self.word_cut_at.is_some(),
  1083	            presence: self.presence_unknown,

  1105	    fn stream_aggregate_scoped(&mut self, scan: &Scan, mut aggregate: super::extrema::Aggregate<'_>) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
  1106	        self.schema.refuse_private(&scan.table)?;

  1109	        let index = &table.indexes[&scan.index];
  1110	        let bounds = self.bounds(scan, table, index, Some(&prefix))?;
  1111	        self.page_depth += 1;

  1113	        self.page_depth -= 1;

  1116	            let prefix = self.prefix_values(scan)?;

  1118	            self.horizon_coverage(scan, &prefix, retained);
  1119	        }

     1	//! The engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2).
     2	//!
     3	//! **The model side.** Each case's surface program goes through
     4	//! `snapback4_lang::emit::lean` (the parsed tree, never the binder's IR) to
     5	//! the model's case JSON, and through `quarry-oracle` (`snapback4/lean`,
     6	//! `lake build`), one long-lived process per worker that answers one tagged
     7	//! outcome per line. A case the emitter cannot translate is `Unsupported`
     8	//! under the emitter's named reason, never a pass.
     9	//!
    10	//! **The engine side.** The engine over SQLite (the ordinary in-memory
    11	//! server store) at `Ceilings::default()`, the ceilings a production server
    12	//! runs, not the case's stored budget; the model runs unbounded (its meter is
    13	//! abstract, OPEN M1).
    14	//!
    15	//! **Compared fields** (README, "How the harness compares"):
    16	//!
    17	//! - `kind`: answer, unavailable, refused, committed;
    18	//! - `code`: the refusal code (sites are not modelled);
    19	//! - `data` / `result`: exact, both sides as tagged values, so an id never
    20	//!   compares equal to a text (OPEN V1); rows compare as field maps;
    21	//! - `complete`: exact;
    22	//! - `next`: presence only (OPEN C1). When both carry one, each side
    23	//!   continues from its own cursor and the next page is compared
    24	//!   (`continuation-*`), until both are exhausted;
    25	//! - a mutation then runs `observe` on each side's resulting store
    26	//!   (`post-*`): the engine's store, and the model's committed store (or the
    27	//!   original store after a refusal).
    28	//!
    29	//! Meter fields, cut sites, `tables` and `seq` are not compared. Expiry
    30	//! sweeps advance both stores before the following program is compared.
    31	//!
    32	//! **Signatures** are `lean--{field}--{engine}~{model}`, each side's stable
    33	//! class (a refusal code, `unavailable`, `committed`, or the data's shape).
    34	//! The finding's `left` is the engine's raw record, `right` the model's raw
    35	//! outcome; the canonical forms compared are its `continuation`.
    36	// @ref LLP 3000.035#52-stage-2-a-small-lean-core — the Lean model as a further oracle over the Stage 1 generator.
    37	use crate::{
    38	    ast::Body,
    39	    campaign::Report,
    40	    execute::{self, Outcome},
    41	    generate::Case,
    42	    oracle::{Finding, Oracle, Verdict},
    43	};
    44	use serde_json::{Map, Value as Json, json};
    45	use snapback4_core::Ceilings;
    46	use snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanModule;
    47	use std::{
    48	    cell::RefCell,
    49	    collections::BTreeSet,
    50	    fs::{self, OpenOptions},
    51	    io::{BufRead, BufReader, Write},
    52	    path::PathBuf,
    53	    process::{Child, ChildStdin, ChildStdout, Command, Stdio},
    54	};
    55	
    56	/// The `quarry-oracle` executable: `SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE`, else the Lake
    57	/// build in this checkout.
    58	pub fn oracle_path() -> PathBuf {
    59	    match std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE") {
    60	        Some(p) => PathBuf::from(p),
    61	        None => PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("../../lean/.lake/build/bin/quarry-oracle"),
    62	    }
    63	}
    64	
    65	/// Bring this checkout's `quarry-oracle` up to date with its model sources
    66	/// (`lake build quarry-oracle`, under a second when current). A merge or
    67	/// checkout that changes `lean/` leaves the previous executable in `.lake`,
    68	/// and the harness would report that older model's choices as disagreements
    69	/// (2026-10-12: a stale pre-guard oracle on the merged `sb4-loop` tree showed
    70	/// `lean--complete--complete~incomplete`). Nothing is done when
    71	/// `SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE` names the oracle (the caller vouches for it; a
    72	/// campaign's workers inherit the refreshed path that way) or when `lake` is
    73	/// not installed (a missing oracle still refuses at `LeanProcess::spawn`).
    74	pub fn refresh() -> Result<(), String> {
    75	    if std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE").is_some() {
    76	        return Ok(());
    77	    }
    78	    let dir = PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("../../lean");
    79	    // Keep the optional-tool path read-only, including a checkout whose model
    80	    // directory is not writable. A version probe writes no Lake build artifacts.
    81	    match Command::new("lake").arg("--version").current_dir(&dir).stdin(Stdio::null()).output() {
    82	        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
    83	            eprintln!("lake not found: using the Lean oracle as built at {}", oracle_path().display());
    84	            return Ok(());
    85	        }
    86	        Err(e) => return Err(format!("lake: {e}")),
    87	        Ok(_) => {} // The build below reports actual toolchain/configuration errors.
    88	    }
    89	    // @ref LLP 3000.035#52-stage-2-a-small-lean-core — concurrent refreshes must not write the same Lake artifacts.
    90	    let cache = dir.join(".lake");
    91	    fs::create_dir_all(&cache).map_err(|e| format!("Lean refresh cache: {e}"))?;
    92	    let refresh_lock = OpenOptions::new().create(true).truncate(false).read(true).write(true)
    93	        .open(cache.join("snapback4-refresh.lock")).map_err(|e| format!("Lean refresh lock: {e}"))?;
    94	    refresh_lock.lock().map_err(|e| format!("Lean refresh lock: {e}"))?;
    95	    match Command::new("lake").args(["build", "quarry-oracle"]).current_dir(&dir).stdin(Stdio::null()).output() {
    96	        Ok(output) if output.status.success() => Ok(()),
    97	        Ok(output) => Err(format!(
    98	            "`lake build quarry-oracle` failed in {}: {}{}",
    99	            dir.display(),
   100	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout),
   101	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
   102	        )),
   103	        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
   104	            eprintln!("lake not found: using the Lean oracle as built at {}", oracle_path().display());
   105	            Ok(())
   106	        }
   107	        Err(e) => Err(format!("lake: {e}")),
   108	    }
   109	}
   110	
   111	/// One `quarry-oracle` process: a JSON case per line in, an outcome per line out.
   112	pub struct LeanProcess {
   113	    child: Child,
   114	    stdin: ChildStdin,
   115	    stdout: BufReader<ChildStdout>,
   116	}
   117	impl LeanProcess {
   118	    pub fn spawn() -> Result<Self, String> {
   119	        let path = oracle_path();
   120	        if !path.is_file() {
   121	            return Err(format!(
   122	                "Lean oracle missing: {} (run `cd snapback4/lean && lake build`, or set SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE)",
   123	                path.display()
   124	            ));
   125	        }
   126	        let mut child = Command::new(&path)
   127	            .stdin(Stdio::piped())
   128	            .stdout(Stdio::piped())
   129	            .stderr(Stdio::inherit())
   130	            .spawn()
   131	            .map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle {}: {e}", path.display()))?;
   132	        let stdin = child.stdin.take().unwrap();
   133	        let stdout = BufReader::new(child.stdout.take().unwrap());
   134	        Ok(Self { child, stdin, stdout })
   135	    }
   136	    pub fn ask(&mut self, case: &Json) -> Result<Json, String> {
   137	        writeln!(self.stdin, "{case}").map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle write: {e}"))?;
   138	        self.stdin.flush().map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle flush: {e}"))?;
   139	        let mut line = String::new();
   140	        let n = self.stdout.read_line(&mut line).map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle read: {e}"))?;
   141	        if n == 0 {
   142	            return Err("Lean oracle exited".into());
   143	        }
   144	        let answer: Json = serde_json::from_str(&line).map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle answer {line:?}: {e}"))?;
   145	        if answer["kind"] == "error" {
   146	            // A case the model cannot decode is an emitter defect, never a verdict.
   147	            return Err(format!("Lean oracle refused the case: {}", answer["error"]));
   148	        }
   149	        Ok(answer)
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	impl Drop for LeanProcess {
   153	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   154	        let _ = self.child.kill();
   155	        let _ = self.child.wait();
   156	    }
   157	}
   158	
   159	thread_local! {
   160	    static PROCESS: RefCell<Option<LeanProcess>> = const { RefCell::new(None) };
   161	}
   162	
   163	/// Ask this thread's oracle process, starting it on first use and once
   164	/// more if it has died.
   165	pub fn ask(case: &Json) -> Result<Json, String> {
   166	    PROCESS.with(|cell| {
   167	        let mut slot = cell.borrow_mut();
   168	        if slot.is_none() {
   169	            *slot = Some(LeanProcess::spawn()?);
   170	        }
   171	        match slot.as_mut().unwrap().ask(case) {
   172	            Err(e) if e == "Lean oracle exited" => {
   173	                *slot = Some(LeanProcess::spawn()?);
   174	                slot.as_mut().unwrap().ask(case)
   175	            }
   176	            other => other,
   177	        }
   178	    })
   179	}
   180	
   181	/// The engine's value as the model's tagged value; rows as field maps.
   182	pub fn engine_value(v: &snapback4_core::Value) -> Json {
   183	    use snapback4_core::Value as V;
   184	    match v {
   185	        V::Null => json!({"null": null}),
   186	        V::Bool(b) => json!({"bool": b}),
   187	        V::Int(n) => json!({"int": n.to_string()}),
   188	        V::String(s) => json!({"text": s}),
   189	        V::Id(s) => json!({"id": s}),
   190	        V::Bytes(b) => json!({"bytes": b.iter().map(|x| format!("{x:02x}")).collect::<String>()}),
   191	        V::Row(fs) | V::Map(fs) => json!({"row": fs.iter().map(|(k, x)| (k.clone(), engine_value(x))).collect::<Map<_, _>>()}),
   192	        V::Array(xs) => json!({"arr": xs.iter().map(engine_value).collect::<Vec<_>>()}),
   193	    }
   194	}
   195	
   196	/// The model's tagged value with rows as field maps (its rows are ordered pairs).
   197	pub fn model_value(v: &Json) -> Json {
   198	    if let Some(xs) = v.get("arr").and_then(Json::as_array) {
   199	        return json!({"arr": xs.iter().map(model_value).collect::<Vec<_>>()});
   200	    }
   201	    if let Some(fs) = v.get("row").and_then(Json::as_array) {
   202	        let mut m = Map::new();
   203	        for p in fs {
   204	            m.insert(p[0].as_str().unwrap_or_default().to_owned(), model_value(&p[1]));
   205	        }
   206	        return json!({"row": m});
   207	    }
   208	    v.clone()
   209	}
   210	
   211	fn shape(v: &Json) -> String {
   212	    v.as_object().and_then(|o| o.keys().next().cloned()).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".into())
   213	}
   214	
   215	/// One side's comparable record: `kind`, then `code` or `data`/`complete`/`next`.
   216	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
   217	pub struct Canon {
   218	    pub kind: String,
   219	    pub code: Option<String>,
   220	    pub data: Option<Json>,
   221	    pub complete: Option<bool>,
   222	    pub next: bool,
   223	}
   224	impl Canon {
   225	    /// The side's stable class for a differing `field`: the field's own
   226	    /// value where it is a flag, else a code, kind or data shape.
   227	    pub fn class(&self, field: &str) -> String {
   228	        if field.ends_with("complete") {
   229	            return if self.complete == Some(true) { "complete" } else { "incomplete" }.into();
   230	        }
   231	        if field.ends_with("next") {
   232	            return if self.next { "next" } else { "no-next" }.into();
   233	        }
   234	        match (&self.code, &self.data) {
   235	            (Some(code), _) => code.clone(),
   236	            (None, Some(d)) if self.kind == "answer" => shape(d),
   237	            _ => self.kind.clone(),
   238	        }
   239	    }
   240	    fn json(&self) -> Json {
     1	//! Authoring-only source spans. Never part of the schema, IR or device payload.
     2	use crate::{ast::Expr, lexer::{Kind, Token}};
     3	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     4	
     5	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
     6	pub struct SourceMap {
     7	    expressions: BTreeMap<String, (Expr, String, bool, crate::Site)>,
     8	    pub(crate) declarations: BTreeMap<String, String>,
     9	    pub(crate) repairs: Vec<crate::repair::Candidate>,
    10	}
    11	
    12	impl SourceMap {
    13	    pub(crate) fn extend(&mut self, other: &Self) {
    14	        self.expressions.extend(other.expressions.clone());
    15	        self.declarations.extend(other.declarations.clone());
    16	    }
    17	
    18	    pub(crate) fn record(&mut self, expr: &Expr, lines: &[String], tokens: &[Token]) {
    19	        // Block expressions consume layout tokens after their final source
    20	        // token. A dedent's site can already be on the next declaration.
    21	        let Some(end) = tokens.iter().rposition(|t| !matches!(t.kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Indent | Kind::Dedent | Kind::Eof)) else { return };
    22	        let tokens = &tokens[..=end];
    23	        let Some(first) = tokens.first() else { return };
    24	        let Some(last) = tokens.last() else { return };
    25	        let Some(line) = lines.get(last.site.line as usize - 1) else { return };
    26	        let tail: Vec<_> = line.chars().skip(last.site.col as usize - 1).collect();
    27	        let mut length = last.value.chars().count();
    28	        if last.kind == Kind::Str {
    29	            length = 1;
    30	            while length < tail.len() {
    31	                let c = tail[length];
    32	                length += 1;
    33	                if c == tail[0] { break; }
    34	                if c == '\\' { length += 1; }
    35	            }
    36	        }
    37	        let mut text = String::new();
    38	        for number in first.site.line..=last.site.line {
    39	            let Some(line) = lines.get(number as usize - 1) else { return };
    40	            if number != first.site.line { text.push('\n'); }
    41	            let start = if number == first.site.line { first.site.col as usize - 1 } else { 0 };
    42	            let end = if number == last.site.line { last.site.col as usize - 1 + length } else { line.chars().count() };
    43	            text.extend(line.chars().skip(start).take(end.saturating_sub(start)));
    44	        }
    45	        let mut depth = 0_i32;
    46	        let grouped = first.kind == Kind::Symbol && first.value == "(" && tokens.iter().enumerate().all(|(i, t)| {
    47	            if t.kind == Kind::Symbol {
    48	                if t.value == "(" { depth += 1; }
    49	                if t.value == ")" { depth -= 1; }
    50	            }
    51	            depth > 0 || i + 1 == tokens.len()
    52	        }) && depth == 0;
    53	        self.expressions.insert(expr.site().to_string(), (expr.clone(), text, grouped, first.site.clone()));
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    pub(crate) fn get(&self, expr: &Expr) -> Option<(String, bool)> {
    57	        let (syntax, text, grouped, _) = self.expressions.get(&expr.site().to_string())?;
    58	        // A planner may reuse a site while changing the expression.
    59	        (syntax == expr).then(|| (text.clone(), *grouped))
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    pub(crate) fn read(&self, site: &crate::Site) -> Option<(crate::ast::ReadExpr, String, bool, crate::Site)> {
    63	        let (expr, text, grouped, start) = self.expressions.get(&site.to_string())?;
    64	        if let Expr::Read(read) = expr { return Some((read.clone(), text.clone(), *grouped, start.clone())); }
    65	        // Keyed write diagnostics share the read repair verifier. Replace only
    66	        // the target; keep the write kind, condition and payload in its caller.
    67	        let Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } = expr else { return None; };
    68	        let tokens = crate::lexer::lex("", text).0;
    69	        let table_token = tokens.get(1)?;
    70	        let mut depth = 0;
    71	        let close = tokens.iter().skip(2).find(|token| {
    72	            if token.kind != Kind::Symbol { return false; }
    73	            match token.value.as_str() { "[" => depth += 1, "]" => { depth -= 1; if depth == 0 { return true; } }, _ => {} }
    74	            false
    75	        })?;
    76	        let offset = |site: &crate::Site| {
    77	            let lines = text.split_inclusive('\n').collect::<Vec<_>>();
    78	            let line = site.line.checked_sub(1)? as usize;
    79	            Some(lines.get(..line)?.iter().map(|s| s.len()).sum::<usize>() + lines.get(line)?.char_indices()
    80	                .map(|(i, _)| i).nth(site.col.checked_sub(1)? as usize)?)
    81	        };
    82	        let from = offset(&table_token.site)?;
    83	        let end = offset(&close.site)? + 1;
    84	        let mut target_site = start.clone();
    85	        target_site.line += table_token.site.line - 1;
    86	        target_site.col = if table_token.site.line == 1 { start.col + table_token.site.col - 1 } else { table_token.site.col };
    87	        Some((crate::ast::ReadExpr { table: table.clone(), keys: keys.clone(), site: site.clone(), index: None,
    88	            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() },
    89	            text[from..end].into(), false, target_site))
    90	    }
    91	
    92	    pub(crate) fn expression(&self, expr: &Expr) -> String {
    93	        crate::print::with_source(expr, &|expr| self.get(expr))
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /// A comparison's operand needs parentheses around booleans and conditionals.
    97	    pub(crate) fn operand(&self, expr: &Expr) -> String {
    98	        crate::print::with_source_at(expr, 5, &|expr| self.get(expr))
    99	    }
   100	}

   417	
   418	    fn scan_at(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body, position: prefix::Position) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
   419	        self.reject_global_scalar_page(read)?;
   420	        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;

   429	                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_READ_THROUGH_SCAN",
   430	                    format!("{} must pin {} to one readable {} row before enumerating groups", read.table, parent.reference, parent.parent),

For a scalar supply every group key: {}[{}]. Omit an explicit aggregate read rule to inherit parent authorization", parent.parent, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, m.by.join(", "))).guide("maintains"));
   432	            }
   433	        }
   434	        let (range, bound) = self.prefix_bound(read, &index, position)?;

   436	            if prefix.len() != position {
   437	                return Err(super::maintains::refusal(&range.site, format!("{} by {} needs every ranking scope field pinned: {}", read.table, index.name, index.columns[..position].join(", ")),
   438	                    format!("pin {} with where equalities, then read {} last {} by .value after c; omit the scope predicate for a global ranking", index.columns[..position].join(", "), read.table, range.take)));
   439	            }
   440	        }
   441	        if range.take == 0 {
   442	            return Err(type_error(&range.site, "a page holds at least one row", print::read_expr(&ReadExpr { range: Some(ast::RangeSpec { take: 50, ..range.clone() }), ..read.clone() })));
   443	        }
   444	        let mut page_source = read.clone();
   445	        page_source.computed.extend(index.computed.iter().map(|(key, value)| {
   446	            let mut value = value.clone();
   447	            if let A::Call { args, .. } = &mut value

   451	            (key.clone(), value)

   454	            key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone());

   612	
   613	/// A stable feature name for a residual conjunct of `generate`.
   614	fn residual_name(residual: &str) -> &'static str {
   615	    match residual {
   616	        ".score >= 0" => "score-ge",
   617	        ".label != ''" => "label-ne",
   618	        ".maybe = null" => "nullable-eq-null",
   619	        ".maybe = n" => "nullable-eq-arg",
   620	        "(.score > 0 or .flag = false)" => "disjunction",
   621	        _ => "other",
   622	    }
   623	}
   624	
   625	/// One case, counted into `report`.
   626	pub fn check(case: &Case, report: &mut Report, mut found: impl FnMut(crate::oracle::Finding)) -> Result<(), String> {
   627	    report.generated += 1;
   628	    for feature in crate::coverage::features(case) {
   629	        *report.coverage.entry(feature).or_default() += 1;
   630	    }
   631	    let source = case.program.source();
   632	    if let Err(e) = execute::compile(&source) {
   633	        report.binder_refused_unexpected += 1;
   634	        return Err(format!("binder: {e}"));
   635	    }
   636	    report.compiled += 1;
   637	    let checked = compare(case, &source)?;
   638	    report.executed += checked.executed;
   639	    if let Verdict::Pass = &checked.verdict {
   640	        // What agreed, by outcome class: evidence for the open points.
   641	        *report.store_stats.entry(format!("lean-agreed/{}", checked.class)).or_default() += 1;
   642	    }
   643	    let stats = &mut report.store_stats;
   644	    // @ref LLP 3000.040#25-the-lean-model — calendar evidence is counted where it is compared.
   645	    if case.program.time_arg {
   646	        *stats.entry("lean-calendar-generated".into()).or_default() += 1;
   647	        let class = match &checked.verdict { Verdict::Unsupported(_) => "unsupported", Verdict::Pass => "agreed", Verdict::Disagree(_) => "disagreeing", _ => "other" };
   648	        *stats.entry(format!("lean-calendar-{class}")).or_default() += 1;
   649	    }
   650	    match &checked.verdict {
   651	        Verdict::Unsupported(_) => *stats.entry("lean-unsupported".into()).or_default() += 1,
   652	        _ => {
   653	            *stats.entry("lean-supported".into()).or_default() += 1;
   654	            *stats.entry(format!("lean-supported-{}", if checked.mutation { "mutation" } else { "query" })).or_default() += 1;
   655	            *stats.entry("lean-pages-compared".into()).or_default() += checked.pages;
   656	            if checked.pages > 1 {
   657	                *stats.entry("lean-continued-reads".into()).or_default() += 1;
   658	            }
   659	        }
   660	    }
   661	    if !matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Unsupported(_)) {
   662	        let disagreed = matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Disagree(_));
   663	        for feature in features(case) {
   664	            *stats.entry(format!("lean-feature/{feature}/compared")).or_default() += 1;
   665	            if disagreed {
   666	                *stats.entry(format!("lean-feature/{feature}/disagreeing")).or_default() += 1;
   667	            }
   668	        }
   669	    }
   670	    report.by_oracle.entry("lean".into()).or_default().record(&checked.verdict);
   671	    if let Verdict::Disagree(f) = checked.verdict {
   672	        found(f);
   673	    }
   674	    Ok(())
   675	}
   676	
   677	pub fn reproduce(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<Verdict, String> {
   678	    execute::compile(source).map_err(|e| format!("binder: {e}"))?;
   679	    compare(case, source).map(|c| c.verdict)
   680	}
   681	
   682	/// The Lean campaign's case for `seed`: the Stage 1 generator's case, and
   683	/// for about half the seeds a variant aimed at the model's open points
   684	/// (`snapback4/lean/OPEN.md`), from an independent stream so the base case
   685	/// is unchanged:
   686	///
   687	/// - per-effect rules on `items` (R2's named and unnamed update rules, W1–W4
   688	///   precedence between rules, readability and clashes, C8's non-uniform
   689	///   reads), with `online only` so any read rule is admissible;
   690	/// - `access` unreadable to everyone (R1: whether a rule probe sees a row its
   691	///   table hides);
   692	/// - page orders over a nullable, a boolean or a unique id column (V2, V3);
   693	/// - a further domain conjunct: residual filters (C2's lookahead) and a
   694	///   null-valued equality (V5).
   695	///
   696	/// A third independent stream (so the draws above are unchanged) adds the
   697	/// forms the engine decides per caller (OPEN C5, C8):
   698	///
   699	/// - half the unpaged totals become a search `exists` over the same domain
   700	///   (LLP 3000.036 §15.12), which needs no decided rule and so draws every
   701	///   read rule;
   702	/// - a third of `Owner`/`OwnerOrWitness` read rules become
   703	///   `.owner = viewer or exists access[0, viewer]`, a readable grant (§12.1);
   704	///   `OwnerOrWitness` under the always-pinned `.bucket` is the parent-pinned
   705	///   grant (§16.3);
   706	/// - a third of pages and `exists` reads end their domain with a test that
   707	///   reads no row (`exists access[bucket, viewer]`, its negation, `a < b`),
   708	///   which the compiler lowers to an `if` around a returned page (§15.4).
   709	pub fn generate(seed: u64) -> Case {
   710	    use crate::ast::{Read, Rule};
   711	    let mut case = crate::generate::generate(seed);
   712	    unbound(&mut case);
   713	    // Half the widened cases the model cannot run (projections, expiry) are
   714	    // narrowed back into its fragment; the other half stay named unsupported.
   715	    if crate::generate::Rng::new(seed ^ 0x4c45_414e_4e41_5257).next() % 2 == 0 {
   716	        narrow(&mut case);
   717	    }
   718	    let mut extra = crate::generate::Rng::new(seed ^ 0x4c45_414e_4558_5354);
   719	    if let Body::Read { kind: kind @ (Read::Count | Read::Sum | Read::Min | Read::Max), .. } = &mut case.program.body {
   720	        if extra.pick(2) == 0 {
   721	            *kind = Read::Exists;
   722	        }
   723	    }
   724	    variants(&mut case, seed);
   725	    if let Some(effects) = &mut case.program.tables[0].effects {
   726	        if matches!(effects[0], Rule::Owner | Rule::OwnerOrWitness) && extra.pick(3) == 0 {
   727	            effects[0] = Rule::OwnerOrGrant;
   728	        }
   729	    }
   730	    if matches!(case.program.body, Body::Read { kind: Read::Page | Read::Bounded | Read::Exists, .. }) && extra.pick(3) == 0 {
   731	        let guards = ["exists access[bucket, viewer]", "not exists access[bucket, viewer]", "a < b"];
   732	        case.program.guard = Some(guards[extra.pick(3) as usize].to_owned());
   733	    }
   734	    membership_page(&mut case, seed);
   735	    boolean_grant_page(&mut case, seed);
   736	    crate::generate::page3(&mut case);
   737	    crate::generate::page4(&mut case);
   738	    case
   739	}
   740	
   741	/// An unpinned owner-or-membership page. Half the rosters retain cap 16 by the
   742	/// fixed principal; half rely on the total unique membership point. Both use
   743	/// the inverse `(principal, bucket)` index for metered discovery before merge.
   744	fn membership_page(case: &mut Case, seed: u64) {
   745	    use crate::ast::{Read, Residency, Rule};
   746	    let mut rng = crate::generate::Rng::new(seed ^ 0x4d45_4d42_4552_5047);
   747	    let inherited_id = case.program.tables.iter().find(|table| table.name == "access")
   748	        .is_some_and(|table| table.columns.iter().any(|column| column.name == "item"));
   749	    let inherited_discovery = case.program.tables.iter().find(|table| table.name == "access")
   750	        .is_some_and(|table| table.keys.iter().any(|(name, _, _)| name == "byPrincipalOnly"));
   751	    if inherited_id || inherited_discovery {
   752	        let read = if inherited_id { Rule::OwnerOrIdWitness } else { Rule::OwnerOrWitness };
   753	        case.program.tables[0].rule = read.clone();
   754	        case.program.tables[0].effects = Some(vec![read, Rule::Allow, Rule::Allow, Rule::Allow]);
   755	        if inherited_id && case.program.guard.as_deref().is_some_and(|guard| guard.contains("access[")) {
   756	            case.program.guard = Some("a < b".into());
   757	        }
   758	        return
   759	    }
   760	    let Body::Read { kind: kind @ (Read::Page | Read::Bounded), filter, owner, project, .. } = &mut case.program.body else { return };
   761	    if rng.pick(8) != 0 { return }
   762	    *kind = Read::Page;
   763	    *filter = false;
   764	    *owner = false;
   765	    *project = None;
   766	    let items = &mut case.program.tables[0];
   767	    let by_id = rng.pick(2) == 0;
   768	    items.rule = if by_id { Rule::OwnerOrIdWitness } else { Rule::OwnerOrWitness };
   769	    items.effects = None;
   770	    items.residency = Residency::Online;
   390	fn owner_membership_shape(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
   391	    let Some(table) = tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
   392	    let Some(rule) = read_rule(table) else { return false };
   393	    let mut leaves = Vec::new(); disjuncts(rule, &mut leaves);
   394	    let party = leaves.iter().any(|leaf| self::party(table, leaf).is_some());
   395	    let membership = leaves.iter().any(|leaf| membership_probe_point(tables, synthesized, table, leaf));
   396	    party && membership && leaves.iter().all(|leaf| self::party(table, leaf).is_some() || membership_probe_point(tables, synthesized, table, leaf))
   397	}
   398	
   399	fn membership_probe_point(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, focal: &TableInfo, predicate: &Expr) -> bool {
   400	    let Expr::Exists { read, .. } = predicate else { return false };
   401	    // PAGE reserves only an exact point with one target-row component. A
   402	    // fully fixed probe is an ordinary static grant and remains eligible for
   403	    // the older branch merge.
   404	    if membership_target(predicate).is_none() { return false; }
   405	    let Some(table) = tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
   406	    let Some(columns) = super::intent_lanes::probe_columns(tables, synthesized, focal, read) else { return false };
   407	    let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
   408	    // A byId parent-owner probe remains an ordinary page guard when the
   409	    // outer id/reference is already pinned. Reserve the composite membership
   410	    // points whose inverted discovery belongs to PAGE.
   411	    table.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns != ["id"])
   412	        .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name))
   413	        .any(|index| if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| columns.contains(column)) } else { index.columns == columns })
   414	}
   415	
   416	/// `exists t where W` with `W` branch-decided: `exists (branch 1) or …`, each
   417	/// a decided existence read, so the answer is `true` or `false`. A rule with a
   418	/// grant keeps its single read (a grant-guarded existence lane is not planned).
   419	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#8 — exists over the same shapes, without grants.
   420	pub(super) fn exists(tables: &Tables, requests: &mut Requests, read: &ReadExpr, site: &Site) -> Option<Expr> {
   421	    if read.range.is_some() { return None; }
   422	    let branches = branches(tables, &Default::default(), requests, read, false)?;
   423	    let mut candidate = requests.clone();
   424	    let errors = candidate.error_count();
   425	    let mut reads = Vec::with_capacity(branches.len());
   426	    for (conjuncts, _) in branches {
   427	        let mut lane = read.clone();
   428	        lane.filter = intent::conjunction(conjuncts);
   429	        candidate.read(tables, &mut lane, false);
   430	        if !decided(tables, &lane) || !candidate.branch_index_fits(&tables[&read.table], &lane) { return None; }
   431	        reads.push(Expr::Exists { read: lane, site: site.clone() });
   432	    }
   433	    if candidate.error_count() != errors { return None; }
   434	    *requests = candidate;
   435	    reads.into_iter().reduce(|left, right| Expr::Binary { op: "or".into(), site: site.clone(), left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right) })
   436	}
   437	
   438	/// The branches of an undecided read, or `None` when the rule does not apply.
   439	fn branches(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &Requests, read: &ReadExpr, grants: bool) -> Option<Vec<Branch>> {
   440	    let table = tables.get(&read.table)?;
   441	    if read.index.is_some() || !read.keys.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.computed.is_empty() || read.cursor_prefix.is_some() { return None; }
   442	    if table.maintained.is_some() || table.through.is_some() || table.decl.ephemeral.is_some() || !table.captures.is_empty() || table.decl.public.is_some() { return None; }
   443	    let rule = read_rule(table)?;
   444	    // A page its own pins decide keeps its plan.
   445	    let mut probe = requests.clone();
   446	    let mut planned = read.clone();
   447	    probe.read(tables, &mut planned, false);
   448	    if decided(tables, &planned) { return None; }
   449	    // Each lane evaluates the predicate's parts on its own rows: they must be
   450	    // total, so moving an alternative into its own lane cannot raise a refusal
   451	    // that the original's short circuit skipped (`or (… and 1 / n = 1)`).
   452	    if read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| !super::intent_compound::total_readable(tables, synthesized, table, filter)) { return None; }
   453	    let conjuncts = read.filter.as_ref().map(|filter| { let mut out = Vec::new(); intent::split(*filter.clone(), &mut out); out }).unwrap_or_default();
   454	    let alternatives = where_branches(table, &conjuncts);
   455	    // Case 1: every alternative of the one `or` in `where` decides the rule.
   456	    if let Some(alternatives) = &alternatives
   457	        && alternatives.iter().all(|alternative| {
   458	            let mut lane = read.clone();
   459	            lane.filter = intent::conjunction(alternative.clone());
   460	            let mut probe = requests.clone();
   461	            probe.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane);
   462	            decided(tables, &lane)
   463	        }) {
   464	        return Some(alternatives.iter().map(|alternative| (alternative.clone(), false)).collect());
   465	    }
   466	    // Case 2: the read rule is parties and page guards: readable grants, and
   467	    // disjuncts the page's own pins decide (LLP 3000.036 §16.3), which the
   468	    // binding check confirms with the core's `covering_grant`.
   469	    let mut disjunct_list = Vec::new();
   470	    disjuncts(rule, &mut disjunct_list);
   471	    if disjunct_list.len() < 2 { return None; }
   472	    let pinned: Vec<String> = conjuncts.iter().filter_map(|conjunct| intent::equality(conjunct, table).map(|(column, _)| column)).collect();
   473	    let mut parties = Vec::new();
   474	    for disjunct in &disjunct_list {
   475	        if let Some(column) = party(table, disjunct) { if !parties.contains(&column) { parties.push(column); } }
   476	        else if columns_within(disjunct, &pinned) { /* a guard: the grant lane reads it */ }
   477	        else { return None; }
   478	    }
   479	    // `exists` takes case 1 only: an `or` of party reads misses rows for the
   480	    // system, which every rule admits, and a guard lane has no existence form.
   481	    if parties.is_empty() || !grants { return None; }
   482	    let base = alternatives.unwrap_or_else(|| vec![conjuncts.clone()]);
   483	    // The grant lane, the read as written, is read for a caller its guards
   484	    // admit and for the system (which every rule admits); a rule of parties
   485	    // alone has no guard, so the lane is the system's only.
   486	    let mut out = vec![(conjuncts, true)];
   487	    for branch in &base {
   488	        for column in &parties {
   489	            let mut lane = vec![viewer_pin(column, &read.site)];
   490	            lane.extend(branch.iter().cloned());
   491	            out.push((lane, false));
   492	        }
   493	    }
   494	    (out.len() <= MAX_BRANCHES).then_some(out)
   495	}
   496	
   497	/// The bounded positive alternatives of the read's top-level conjunction.
   498	/// Each `or` contributes a Cartesian factor, so
   499	/// `(a = x or b = x) and (state = 'open' or state = 'held')` becomes four
   500	/// independently pinned lanes.  Keep the published eight-lane ceiling while
   501	/// allowing an exact query predicate to narrow a wider read-rule union.
   502	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact positive query scopes may combine party and status alternatives.
   503	fn where_branches(table: &TableInfo, conjuncts: &[Expr]) -> Option<Vec<Vec<Expr>>> {
   504	    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
   505	    let mut distributed = false;
   506	    for conjunct in conjuncts {
   507	        let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
   508	        disjuncts(conjunct, &mut alternatives);
   509	        distributed |= alternatives.len() > 1;
   510	        if branches.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > MAX_BRANCHES { return None; }
   511	        branches = branches.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |alternative| {
   512	            let mut lane = prefix.clone();
   513	            intent::split(alternative.clone(), &mut lane);
   514	            lane
   515	        })).collect();
   516	    }
   517	    if !distributed || branches.len() < 2 || branches.iter().any(|lane| !lane.iter().any(|conjunct| pin(table, conjunct))) {
   518	        return None;
   519	    }
   520	    Some(branches)
   521	}
   522	
   523	/// An equality the planner turns into an index prefix. A nullable column is
   524	/// pinned only to `viewer`, never to a value that may be null, so a branch
   525	/// never seeks a null the original filter rejects.
   526	fn pin(table: &TableInfo, conjunct: &Expr) -> bool {
   527	    let Some((column, value)) = intent::equality(conjunct, table) else { return false };
   528	    column != "id" && table.columns.get(&column).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.optional || is_viewer(&value))
   529	}
   530	
   531	/// The planned read's prefix decides the read rule: every column the rule
   532	/// reads is pinned (with the self-witness proof), or the rule is true by the
   533	/// pins' form (the core's `rule_true`, and the Lean model's `Rule.byForm`).
   534	pub(super) fn decided(tables: &Tables, planned: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
   535	    let Some(table) = tables.get(&planned.table) else { return false };
   536	    if super::intent_lanes::prefix_decides_rule(tables, planned) { return true; }
   537	    read_rule(table).is_some_and(|rule| by_form(rule, planned))
   538	}
   539	
   540	/// `allow`, `.col = viewer` pinned to `viewer`, `.col = 'x'` pinned to `'x'`,
   541	/// and `or`/`and` of those.
   542	pub(super) fn by_form(rule: &Expr, planned: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
   543	    let pinned = |column: &str| planned.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_deref() == Some(column)).map(|key| &key.value);
   544	    match rule {
   545	        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "allow" => true,
   546	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => by_form(left, planned) || by_form(right, planned),
   547	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => by_form(left, planned) && by_form(right, planned),
   548	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" => {
   549	            let (column, value) = match (intent::column(left), intent::column(right)) {
   550	                (Some(column), None) => (column, right.as_ref()),
   551	                (None, Some(column)) => (column, left.as_ref()),
   552	                _ => return false,
   553	            };
   554	            match (value, pinned(column)) {
   555	                (value, Some(pin)) if is_viewer(value) => is_viewer(pin),
   556	                (Expr::Literal { value, .. }, Some(Expr::Literal { value: pin, .. })) => value == pin && *value != Literal::Null,
   557	                _ => false,
   558	            }
   559	        }
   560	        _ => false,

   316	
   317	/// What one case comes to; `executed` counts engine and model calls.
   318	pub struct Checked {
   319	    pub verdict: Verdict,
   320	    pub executed: u64,
   321	    pub pages: u64,
   322	    pub mutation: bool,
   323	    /// The first outcome's class on an agreeing case (`committed`, a code,
   324	    /// `unavailable`, or the data's shape).
   325	    pub class: String,
   326	}
   327	
   328	/// The module, or why the case is outside the fragment.
   329	pub fn module(source: &str) -> Result<LeanModule, String> {
   330	    let (document, diagnostics) = snapback4_lang::parse_document("schema.q", source);
   331	    if !diagnostics.is_empty() {
   332	        return Err("parse".into());
   333	    }
   334	    snapback4_lang::emit::lean::from_documents(&[document]).map_err(|u| u.reason)
   335	}
   336	
   337	/// Compare one case whose source compiles. `Err` is a harness or emitter
   338	/// defect (never a verdict).
   339	pub fn compare(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<Checked, String> {
   340	    let unsupported = |reason: String| Ok(Checked { verdict: Verdict::Unsupported(reason), executed: 0, pages: 0, mutation: false, class: String::new() });
   341	    let module = match module(source) {
   342	        Ok(m) => m,
   343	        Err(reason) => return unsupported(reason),
   344	    };
   345	    let mutation = case.program.mutation();
   346	    if let Err(u) = module.program("run") {
   347	        return unsupported(u.reason);
   348	    }
   349	    if mutation {
   350	        if let Err(u) = module.program("observe") {
   351	            return unsupported(format!("observe-{}", u.reason));
   352	        }
   353	    }
   354	    let store = match module.store(&case.rows) {
   355	        Ok(s) => s,
   356	        Err(u) => return unsupported(u.reason),
   357	    };
   358	    let mut m_args = match module.tag_args("run", &case.args) {
   359	        Ok(a) => a,
   360	        Err(u) => return unsupported(u.reason),
   361	    };
   362	    // The prelude: each step on the engine and on the model's store, in order.
   363	    for i in 0..case.steps.len() {
   364	        if let Err(u) = module.program(&format!("prep{i}")) {
   365	            return unsupported(format!("prelude-{}", u.reason));
   366	        }
   367	        if let Err(u) = module.tag_args(&format!("prep{i}"), &case.steps[i].args) {
   368	            return unsupported(format!("prelude-{}", u.reason));
   369	        }
   370	    }
   371	    let (mut engine, prelude) = execute::world(case, source)?;
   372	    engine.set_ceilings(Ceilings::default());
   373	    let mut executed = 0;
   374	    let mut pages = 0;
   375	    let mut store = store;
   376	    let mut steps = prelude.iter();
   377	    let mut trail = vec![];
   378	    for (i, step) in case.steps.iter().enumerate() {
   379	        let name = format!("prep{i}");
   380	        if step.sweep {
   381	            let e_sweep = steps.next().ok_or("prelude sweep outcome missing")?;
   382	            if e_sweep.kind != "sweep" || e_sweep.value.get("error").is_some() {
   383	                return Err(format!("engine prelude sweep failed: {:?}", e_sweep.value));
   384	            }
   385	            let args = module.tag_args(&name, &step.args).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?;
   386	            let viewer = step.viewer.as_deref().unwrap_or(&case.principal);
   387	            let swept = ask(&module.case_at_sweep(&name, store.clone(), viewer, args, None, step.at, true).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   388	            executed += 1;
   389	            if swept["kind"] != "committed" { return Err(format!("Lean prelude sweep failed: {swept}")); }
   390	            store = swept["store"].clone();
   391	        }
   392	        let e_raw = steps.next().ok_or("prelude outcome missing")?.clone();
   393	        let args = module.tag_args(&name, &step.args).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?;
   394	        let viewer = step.viewer.as_deref().unwrap_or(&case.principal);

The heap ledger applies to the whole membership merge, including owner and
system lane buffers as well as dynamic target buffers. It reserves source
distinct sets, evaluated prefixes and bounds, merge-position and heap slots,
deduplication, the emitted-page vector, frontier decode, and cursor/page-record
scratch before allocation. A source distinct key is first sized by the
allocation-free canonical JSON writer, then charged and encoded into one
bounded buffer of exactly that requested capacity; escaped parent text cannot
cause geometric growth after admission. Compiled scans remain shared; dynamic
descriptors borrow one compiled target scan rather than cloning its filter tree
per parent.

On a device the membership source and every opened target range retain their
normal per-scan coverage decisions. PAGE is an explicit exception to §7's
blanket synced-union exclusion for this proved source shape: it has a
system-only lane rather than a probe-grant lane, so acquired membership and
target union groups can use the same plan and produce the same complete page
as the server. An online or unacquired source makes discovery unknown and
returns the empty incomplete answer above. An unheld or horizon-incomplete
target range makes the result incomplete and triggers the ordinary server
fallback; no partial device result is marked complete. The plan does not
treat a merely uniform denial as an empty membership set: binding validates
that the source pins decide its read rule true. Expiry from membership
discovery joins target expiry at the existing minimum.

The planner and Lean classifier mirror point-probe resolution before accepting
the branch. A positional probe selects the one declared unique whose ordered
column types accept its arguments; equal-arity keys with different types do not
make it ambiguous, while multiple matching keys still refuse the positional
spelling. Named probe columns are duplicate-free and contain every column of a
duplicate-free declared unique, additional columns are fixed equalities, and a
cap covers the fixed discovery prefix. The descriptor bound counts each party
column once, matching runtime party-lane deduplication while retaining every
distinct membership branch. Its soundness lemma says every emitted
party or parent lane row is admitted under the branch-witness assumption; the
logical theorem does not prove that runtime discovery establishes that
witness. Its coverage lemma splits every admitted row into a party witness or
membership witness. Differential cases include
caps above eight and overlap. Runtime meter regressions cover discovery cuts;
simulator regressions include revocation between pages and missing acquisition;
server and both device stores exercise the same shared-cursor walk.

The implicit `byId` also proves an exact named probe containing `id`; any
additional named components remain required residual equalities. This does not
invent a multi-part positional key: positional probes still resolve through
their typed declared unique, apart from the ordinary single-ID spelling.

A fixed named component may remain an exact equality in the discovery scan's
residual filter rather than its capped prefix, as in
`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]`. PAGE accepts that
component only when the equality is a necessary conjunct over the source row;
an alternative or computed implication is not witness evidence. The retained
filter therefore restricts discovery to `m1`, and a row with the same parent
and principal under another id cannot open a target lane. The planner checks
that every fixed probe component survives as a prefix pin or such a residual
before committing candidate indexes, and the bound-IR validator repeats the
same check without copying the filter. A denial, missing witness, expired
witness, incomplete source horizon or unsupported residual cannot expose an
unproved target lane. Uniform denial and unsupported residuals keep the
ordinary fallback without speculative PAGE indexes. A missing or expired
witness opens no lane under complete discovery, so the owner merge remains
complete; an incomplete source horizon returns the empty incomplete result
described above.

Fixed scalar equalities compare their canonical values across the rule and
expression representations. In particular, unary minus applied to an integer
literal is canonicalized to that negative integer before PAGE validates the
residual and before candidate indexes are committed. Thus `slot = -1` is the
same necessary residual in planning, bound validation and the Lean model; it
does not cause a late fallback that leaves speculative indexes behind.

The intrinsic source `id` is always such a residual, even when fixed. It stays
in the exact logical witness and its equality must hold, but it is not a
discovery-prefix component and cannot satisfy the discovery cap. Thus `cap by
id` alone keeps the ordinary incomplete scan; a separate cap over fixed
discovery columns such as `principal` is required for PAGE.

An optional discovered parent may be `null` only when the exact point probe
identifies the source row without that parent component. For example,
`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` addresses `m1`
through `byId` and checks `parent = null` as a residual equality, so a matching
source row opens one null-parent target range. Discovery uses its ordinary
null-covering physical index, including the stored row-id suffix, and applies
the fixed residual before distinct-parent deduplication. If the varying parent
is instead a component of the probe's nullable unique key, a null argument has
the ordinary point result of no row and cannot open a PAGE lane. The same is
true when any other component of that point key is null. This rule neither
turns nullable uniques into total uniques nor changes their storage or
collision semantics.

A varying source field may be the discovered parent even when it is not the
next physical index component, provided the fixed discovery prefix covers a

may project any field from that row: the implicit `id` in
`groups[id = .parent, principal = viewer]`, or an ordinary `parent` in
`groups[parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` under `unique principal`.
The total unique may be a proper subset of the probe's fixed source columns:
`unique principal` also admits
`groups[parent = .parent, slot = 0, principal = viewer]`. PAGE keeps `slot =
0` as an exact residual on the selected source row; a missing row, `slot =
null`, or any other slot does not open a target range. The provisional wider
discovery index is discarded before requests are committed. When several
covered total uniques exist, PAGE chooses only among points whose prefix

The projected field may be optional under the null-witness rule above. The
source cap, readability, complete-discovery, resource and horizon checks still

The Lean surface gate prices the same physical PAGE candidate before invoking
