## 15. PAGE2: ordinary exact memberships, row ids and parent owners

Wave 10 showed that §14's cap requirement excluded the ordinary membership
schema it was meant to serve:

```quarry
table members:
  parent: parents
  principal: principal
  unique parent, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer

table documents:
  owner: principal
  parent: parents
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
```

`unique parent, principal` proves that the rule's point probe has at most one
witness for one document and caller. It does **not** prove that a principal has
only finitely many membership rows: fixing `principal` leaves arbitrarily many
`parent` values. PAGE2 therefore does not call this source range statically
capped. It admits the exact total-unique witness and runs the inverted
`members where .principal = viewer` discovery under the ordinary read meter.
The §14 discovery fence already gives the required failure semantics: until
the source range is known complete, no owner or membership target row is
emitted; an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or discovery `steps`
cut, unknown device coverage or incomplete horizon returns an empty
`complete: false` page with no continuation. `heap_bytes` exhaustion retains
the existing `E_BOUND` read refusal. Every
distinct value, evaluated prefix and dynamic lane is sized and charged through
§14's heap ledger before allocation, so an uncapped population cannot allocate
past the request ceiling. Retrying a read-meter cut may complete under a larger
budget or smaller population. It cannot skip an undiscovered lane or turn
partial discovery into a negative authorization fact.

This admission proves a safe plan, not completion for every population size.
For a caller with `K` memberships, discovery and target-head work grow with
`K`; once a read ceiling is crossed the fenced result is empty and incomplete,
so a sufficiently large audience can be temporarily unavailable under the
ordinary budget. A cap is therefore optional for correctness and remains the
product's availability bound. If an app must guarantee that the page completes
within a chosen budget, the writer that grants memberships must enforce a cap
on the fixed enrollment group (for example `principal`). A writer allowed to
grant arbitrary memberships controls `K`; point uniqueness alone does not.

A declared cap is a separate static proof only when all of its grouping
columns are fixed by the discovery scan and null exemption cannot apply. Any
maximum is admissible: a cap above the request's examined ceiling may still
cut safely under the preceding rule. A cap grouped by `parent` does not bound a
scan that fixes only `principal`; a cap grouped by `principal` does. A fully
pinned total unique remains the one-row case. Planner, bound-IR validator and
Lean emitter use these same alternatives:

1. the rule probe selects the intrinsic `id` or one declared plain unique,
   and every other probe component is an exact necessary equality;
2. either that selected point is total, the discovery itself is a fully pinned
   total unique, or a declared cap covers the fixed discovery prefix;
3. the fixed pins decide the source read rule true, and all existing index,
   key, label, descriptor, total-predicate and shared-position checks pass.

   252	  | _ => none

   254	/-- The first pin on column `c`: the planner pins each column once. -/
   255	def pinOf (pins : List (String × Expr)) (c : String) : Option Expr :=

   258	def pinnedLit (pins : List (String × Expr)) (c : String) (v : Scalar) : Bool :=

   260	  | some (.lit (.s x)) => decide (x = v)
   261	  | _ => false

   263	def pinnedViewer (pins : List (String × Expr)) (c : String) : Bool :=

   967	    fn write(&self, e: &Expr) -> R<Json> {
   968	        let Expr::Write { op, table, keys, fields, if_absent, condition, .. } = e else { unreachable!() };
   969	        if *op != WriteOp::Insert && keys.is_empty() { return no("row-variable-write", crate::print::expression(e)); }
   970	        let t = self.table(table)?;
   971	        if !t.caps.is_empty() && *op != WriteOp::Delete {
   972	            return no("table-cap", format!("{} writes capped table {table}; the model does not enforce caps", crate::print::expression(e)));
   973	        }
   974	        Ok(match op {
   975	            WriteOp::Insert => {
   976	                if !keys.is_empty() || condition.is_some() {
   977	                    return no("insert-form", crate::print::expression(e));
   978	                }
   979	                json!({"insert": {"table": table, "fields": self.fields(fields, Some(t))?, "ifAbsent": if_absent}})
   980	            }
   981	            WriteOp::Upsert => {
   982	                if *if_absent || condition.is_some() {
   983	                    return no("upsert-form", crate::print::expression(e));
   984	                }
   985	                json!({"upsert": {"table": table, "key": self.key(t, keys)?, "patch": self.fields(fields, Some(t))?}})
   986	            }
   987	            WriteOp::Update => {
   988	                if *if_absent {
   989	                    return no("update-form", crate::print::expression(e));
   990	                }
   991	                let cond = match condition {
   992	                    Some(c) => self.with_focal(table).expr(c)?,
   993	                    None => Json::Null,
   994	                };
   995	                json!({"update": {"table": table, "key": self.key(t, keys)?, "patch": self.fields(fields, Some(t))?, "cond": cond}})
   996	            }
   997	            WriteOp::Delete => {
   998	                if *if_absent || condition.is_some() || !fields.is_empty() {
   999	                    return no("delete-form", crate::print::expression(e));
  1000	                }
  1001	                json!({"delete": {"table": table, "key": self.key(t, keys)?}})
  1002	            }
  1003	        })
  1004	    }

   302	fn equality_filter_note_prints_one_matching_index_and_query() {
   303	    let source = "table skippers:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable logs:\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  public\n  online only\ntable watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer or exists skippers[viewer]\n  online only\nquery myWatches():\n  ignored = logs first 1 by byStart\n  return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart\n";
   304	    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
   305	    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
   306	    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
   307	    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
   308	    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("ignored = logs first 1 by byStart"), "{note}");
   309	    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("return watches[] first 240 by byCrewStarts where .crew = viewer"), "{note}");
   310	
   311	    let rewritten = note.rewrite.split("```quarry\n").nth(1).and_then(|rest| rest.split("\n```").next()).unwrap();
   312	    let query = source.find("query myWatches():").unwrap();
   313	    let repaired = format!("{}{}\n", source[..query].replace("  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer",
   314	        "  by byStart: starts, id\n  by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer"), rewritten);
   315	    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
   316	    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
   317	    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
   318	
   319	    // The old order still fits, but adding the authorization prefix would
   320	    // exceed the portable encoded-key ceiling. Do not print an edit that the
   321	    // compiler itself rejects.
   322	    let wide = source.replace("table watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time", "table watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: text <=430");
   323	    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), wide)]);
   324	    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
   325	    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
   326	    assert!(!note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
   327	}

- `dda0bbdc91:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs:1005` — The equality repair changes only `read.index`, retaining bracket keys that now address different columns. With `byStart: starts, id`, `crew: principal`, and `s: time`, the valid read `watches[s] first 240 by byStart where .crew = viewer` receives `byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id` but retains `watches[s]`. Recompilation interprets `s` as the principal-valued `crew` key and rejects it; matching types could silently change the query’s meaning. Construct the new prefix from the proved equalities while preserving the original keys’ column identities, or omit the repair for incompatible keyed reads. Add an apply-and-recompile regression covering this prefix.
